ParlaMint-AT_1998-07-09-020-XX-NRSITZ-00135_d7e2973	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Traditionally, the Finance and Economic Ministers report to the High House once a year on the economic situation of Austria and the economic aspects of our country. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! It is more than ever necessary to consider the international environment in which our country and our economy have to move. We were and are witnesses of great changes that have profound influences on the economic, working and living conditions in Austria, and will also have in the future. The transformation of the Eastern European states into democracies with market economies, for example, has opened up many new opportunities and prospects. It has the same historical dimension as the enormous progress made in European integration, in particular through economic and monetary union. Finally, the so-called globalisation of the economy also affects the conditions of economic action in Austria. Our policy, Austrian policy, must therefore be increasingly geared to the greatest possible involvement of international framework conditions for our country. The integration of Austria into the European Union and the participation of EMU mean great opportunities for our country, but also a high responsibility in the development of these framework conditions. At the beginning of this week, as the new President-in-Office of the ECOFIN in Brussels, I had the opportunity to present our programme for the Austrian Presidency to my financial ministers from the EU Member States and, of course, to the public. I would also like to take advantage of today's opportunity to inform you, ladies and gentlemen of the Austrian National Council, of my positions represented there. Economic policy issues play an important role in this. At the top of our priorities, we must and will continue and strengthen the common initiatives to combat unemployment and increase employment in the Union. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Of course, we could ask why Austria is considering this issue as a matter of priority, but our country has the second lowest unemployment rate in the European Union. But work is a crucial basis of existence for each and every one and the foundation for true progress towards a social, just and peaceful Europe. And that is our goal, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] National employment action plans have therefore been drawn up on the basis of a Community strategy and are in implementation. The first interim review of the success will be carried out at the Vienna European Council in December of this year. Another task over the next six months will be to prepare for the introduction of the single European currency, the euro. It will be of great importance that, in parallel, we should bring about the necessary intensification of economic policy coordination in the European Union. With the Euro-11 Group, we have set up a discussion forum dedicated in particular to this issue. This group, which was established at the beginning of June under my chairmanship in Luxembourg, met on Monday in Brussels, and we have taken a few steps on this issue. A particular concern must also be to make progress in harmonising European tax policy, although I am aware that this is a particularly difficult task. But: Tax policy is used very strongly as an instrument of location policy, and competition is sometimes led by unfair means. This is neither a question of equality nor a question of adjustment. This is primarily a matter of coordination and, in particular, of important competitive taxes, of minimum standards to ensure fair competition in the European Union. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Finally, in the discussion of the European Commission's proposals for reforming EU policies and the new medium-term financial framework, which are summarised in Agenda 2000, we have undertaken to clarify as many questions as possible, as in the first half of the coming year under the German Presidency, before the elections to the European Parliament, a final political agreement on the future path of the Union will be possible in the next decade. That is why we have the great task of structurally introducing the very difficult discussions in the field of medium-term financing of the European Union under the Austrian Presidency. The Presidency in the European Union thus gives us an opportunity to draw the European Union's common energy more closely on those tasks which are particularly important for the good development of Europe, and hence for us, our economy and the people working in it. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! This scope for design, which traditionally belongs to the Presidency of the European Union, is the special opportunity I have spoken about, an opportunity that we can and will benefit from together. We have to look for European solutions, but they also meet our Austrian concerns. But we must be clear that in a community, not one alone, but all together decide. Ladies and gentlemen! The central concern of our economic policy must therefore be the successful fight against unemployment. For unemployment is a waste of valuable human talents and abilities. Unemployment is a social injustice for the people affected by it. Unemployment and its consequences also burden public budgets. And reducing unemployment is also the key to the sustainable success of the common European currency. [[Further applause with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] I would remind you that the Amsterdam European Council in 1997 included its own chapter on employment in the Treaty on European Union and adopted a resolution on growth and employment. Austrian initiatives – we can be proud of this – have contributed significantly to this. At the Luxembourg Employment Summit, Member States were obliged to draw up a National Employment Action Plan. For Austria, the National Action Plan for Employment was prepared by the Federal Government in intensive cooperation with the social partners. It is a good programme, also because it is based on the common conviction that effective employment policy requires the coordinated use of many different economic policy instruments in order to meet the very diverse and complex causes of unemployment. In particular, we will set the following priorities: First: The National Action Plan aims to further improve the quality of education, which is an increasingly important foundation for good income and employment opportunities for all. Secondly, Further development of the dual training system through the creation of new apprenticeships and more flexible forms of training in response to changes in the requirements for employees in the context of structural change. Thirdly, Developing the innovative labour market policy of the labour market service through additional support measures for training, new jobs in social services, employment foundations and re-entry programmes, which are tailored to the needs of individual groups particularly affected by unemployment. Fourth: Strengthening labour demand, in particular by reducing barriers to access to self-employment and promoting the creation of new jobs, particularly in the health sector. These measures, ladies and gentlemen, are complemented by the modernisation of labour law by the social partners. This should, on the one hand, make it possible to take better account of the changes in the wishes of workers while at the same time increasing the adaptability required. Finally: To improve equal opportunities for women, in particular through the provision of childcare facilities and the reduction of barriers to return to work. A broad programme, an ambitious programme, a programme that will help ensure more employment for Austrians. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Our objective is – this is an ambitious goal – that these measures will create the basis for the creation of some 100 000 additional jobs over the next five years and that the unemployment rate may fall to almost 3.5 percent. This is a goal that we have committed ourselves to with all our possibilities. [[New applause with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Overall, the National Employment Action Plan has succeeded in developing employment policy instruments. At the same time, the basis for coordination between the institutions could be improved, which also need to be implemented by the individual measures. This will help to increase the effectiveness of employment policy as a whole. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The National Action Plan is the result of the interaction of initiatives at European and Austrian level. It is thus not least a successful example of the use of new economic opportunities through our accession to the European Union. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! In fact, the development of employment in Austria shows a partly very encouraging picture, although I do not mention that I am very concerned about unemployment. At the end of June this year, just over 3.1 million people were self-employed in Austria. This is the highest number of employed people we ever had in Austria this month. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] This is hardly known to many people and is not said much too much. We have a record of employees in Austria. The increase in the number of jobs in Austria was thus around 26 000 compared to the same month of the previous year. This means that in Austria, within a year, framework conditions have been created which have allowed 26 000 people to give additional work. It is a pleasure to note that over two thirds of these new jobs are filled with women. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Economic researchers expect employment growth of 32 000 new jobs for this year. Although the number of jobs in Austria is increasing, for many people, but also for some professionals, the number of jobseekers also increases surprisingly and incomprehensiblely. For even more so than the number of new jobs, labour supply is increasing, i.e. the number of those who want to enter the labour market and find employment. In June this was slightly over 200 000 people, around 8,000 more than in June of the previous year. Ladies and gentlemen! This encourages our great efforts to further improve those conditions that will lead to more employment. In all the problems, I would like to point out in particular that last year's youth policies led to a reduction in young people's unemployment. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Austria has the lowest rate of youth unemployment in Europe. And I think this is particularly remarkable and important, because in the initial phase of life, it is important to have a material but also a psychological basis for building life through a job of training or employment. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, economic researchers have been expecting a fall in unemployment for the first time since 1994 for the first time in 1999. So we can see that efforts to create new jobs have been worthwhile! [[New applause with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] However, I do not wish to present just the positive pages here. Of course, there are still tasks and challenges that need to be taken up and solved. In particular, we will have two ways of achieving success, as I believe: on the one hand, an active labour market policy, through which we will promote in particular the skills of job-seekers according to the demand of the economy. The National Employment Action Plan has provided for a number of initiatives, including the necessary additional money. The other way is to further stimulate a robust growth of the economy itself. Agitated by exports and rising domestic demand, the economy is already rising to a relatively high level this year. The growth of our economy was 2.5 percent in the previous year. It is expected to be 3 percent this year, and for the coming year a further growth of 3.2 percent is forecast. Export growth will be around 10 percent in real terms this year and next year and will be higher than import growth, which will also significantly relieve Austria's trade and current account balance. Equipment investment is growing significantly, and a trend change can also be seen in travel. The inflation rate will continue to remain at a historically low level thanks to stability policy. These, ladies and gentlemen, are also those conditions which guarantee the exact implementation of the 1999 budget, which you decided here a few weeks ago. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] As you know, the result will be 2.6 percent of the gross domestic product. Public debt will continue to fall below 65 percent in the coming year. Since 1995, when the federal deficit was 5.2 percent and public debt was close to 70 percent, this has achieved a sustainable improvement in the financial situation of public budgets. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] The basis for this success was the consolidation programme. Not least, it has also found acceptance by its balanced distribution of necessary measures – despite some painful steps. And it has also succeeded in holding back negative effects on growth and employment to the most extent possible. Another feature of this programme is that it has not exhausted itself in short-term and only once effective measures, but has set a significant course for structural reforms. The success of this programme must now be further built on, for a number of good reasons, of which I would like to mention three: First: The cost of interest and fees of the federal financial debt will continue to be over 100 billion schilling annually. Budget consolidation is therefore also necessary in the future in order to make more money available for other more productive tasks. Secondly, Austria has committed itself to the Stability and Growth Pact that has already been discussed. In order to avoid exceeding the deficit limit even in more economicly poor years, the safety upholstery under the 3-percent mark must be sufficiently large. Thirdly, The rapidly changing social and economic conditions also require the adaptation of public services to changing needs. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The increasing international competition makes the quality of public services an increasingly important location factor. That is to say, we must continue to strive to reduce the end of public budgets in Austria – a measure that is unanimously recommended to us by many well-known international institutions, not least by the International Monetary Fund, and an approach to which the agreements in the European Union, in the interest of the stability of the future European Economic and Monetary Area, are also meaningful. One important aspect, ladies and gentlemen, which I would like to highlight in particular is the increased need for financial policy coordination in our own country, i.e. between local authorities. For the target sizes for public budgets at European level apply to the general state, so they are not only formulated for the federal government alone. Against this background, the Federal Government, the Länder and the municipalities have already worked together successfully so far and have now agreed on a consultation mechanism at the political level. The aim is to prevent laws or regulations of one local authority from burdening another against their will. What is still missing is a Stability Pact, which internally divides the deficit and debt ratios between the local authorities. This will be negotiated in autumn – I assume. In general, I believe that this is very important, it will be even more important in the future than in the past, that all public budgets should follow the principles of economic, efficient and effective budgetary management in order to ensure that Austria can sufficiently perceive its opportunities in the framework of the European partnership. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] In this context, I would like to point out now that the negotiations on the financial compensation for the period after the turn of the millennium, i.e. from 2001, must be marked by this principle of overall responsibility for the finances of the general state. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! The 1996/1997 consolidation programme saved government expenditure on the one hand, and, of course, revenue increased. This is also reflected in the slightly increased tax and tax rate, although I must stress that more than half of this quota does not benefit the Federal Government, but rather the countries and municipalities, and above all also social security schemes and, to a small extent, the European Union. So what has sometimes been heard in recent weeks, namely that taxpayers would work almost half of their time for the Minister of Finance, is not true. The citizens work for themselves. They work for themselves and for their quality of life. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] [[Intervocations with the liberals.]] – I am convinced that you will say this from the speaker’s desk, and then I can comment on it. I don't understand you now. For example, citizens will not even be able to finance an international educational system with their taxes and taxes – for example – that will not even challenge you. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] They finance a social security system that guarantees them good care in the event of unemployment, accident or illness. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! They finance a pension scheme that is one of the best in the world. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] [[Further calls to the liberals.]] In no other country is there such generous support for families and as good care for people in need of care as we do in Austria. And we confess to that. [[New applause with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Security in our country is also far higher than elsewhere. The federal states also offer very generous housing promotion systems. Another thing, my dear ones. Ladies and gentlemen: Especially when you come to other European cities, you can see – and this is very positive – that Austria's cities and communities are beautiful, clean and worth living thanks to the well-functioning local services. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] [[Intervocations with the liberals.]] All these advantages together form important location factors for Austria. I do not know why you laugh when I say that the cities are safe and exemplary. This is not funny at all, but a fact, a statement that we can be proud of in Austria. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] In Austria, no one, neither in Vienna nor elsewhere, needs to be afraid when he walks in the evening, as is the case in other cities of Europe, even in the European capital Brussels. I would like to point this out in detail. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] That can't be discussed away, even if it doesn't fit you. And this is also a result of the policy for which this federal government stands. [[New applause with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I am not saying all of this because of self-indulgence, but that is the facts. I am saying this because any change in government revenue will naturally have an impact on the performance side. Any structural reform must therefore also be based on serious and precise considerations. This is very important when it comes to a change in tax policy. I say once again, so that no mistake arises, what I have said several times here in this House: The Austrian Federal Government is preparing a smart, balanced tax reform for the year 2000. Austria's best experts in this field are currently testing without delay, where changes are necessary and appropriate for material reasons. It is a complex project which must meet several objectives. On the one hand, we are striving for a more environmentally friendly distribution of the tax burden. This is to be achieved by reducing the burden on labour and increasing the burden on resource consumption. The discharge of the labour factor is an objective that is being pursued throughout Europe. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] On the other hand, the political scope of the public authorities must be ensured so that our comprehensive and exemplary system of social security, investment and employment policy, security and education policy can be ensured in the future, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] We must therefore address this tax reform with great responsibility for the stability of public budgets, but also for the whole development in our country. We have already taken a first step towards this reform with the Family Tax Reform. In this context, however, it was also to be heard that we were a little too generous. That is why I am calling for a major, structural-political and budgetary reform. What I certainly do not want – and I am saying this quite clearly – is a tax reform of the good hope, at the end of which a savings package number 3 should stand. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] To think only about tax reductions would therefore be completely inadequate and one-sided. In the interests of the development and justice of our tax system, we must take a step further and deal much more with the question of a reasonable and fair distribution of taxes in our country and in our society. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] This includes both the aspect of tax reductions and employment on the question of how the level of taxation can influence developments in our society. In the sense of the joint responsibility of the Federal Government, the countries and the municipalities, the taxes in their area will also be included by the Tax Reform Commission and the discussion on the new financial compensation. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! At this point, I would like to return to the interplay of economic policy within the framework of the responsibilities of the Member States of the European Union and of policies at Community level. We are just a few months before the start of the third stage of economic and monetary union. The new single European currency opens up enormous economic opportunities and opportunities. It will remove the internal market from fluctuations in exchange rates in the future. Calculated economic conditions are an important basis for greater investment, growth and employment. Competition within Europe is thus becoming fairer and more transparent. An essential area, which is important for the functioning of the Union as a whole and the internal market, is therefore also the coordination of Member States' tax policies and thus the fight against harmful tax competition. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Tax policy is an instrument of location policy. It also works with unfair means. On the one hand, we must, in particular, counteract the harmful tax competition affecting the fiscal efficiency of national tax systems and, on the other hand, the performance of public budgets, on the other hand, we must reduce the taxation of the real factors of production, at the expense of others, such as capital and energy, if we want to achieve that the economy in Europe does not grow unilaterally, without employment, and that growth does not sacrifice social security systems. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Highly esteemed ladies and gentlemen! Austria's economy is on a robust growth path. We will achieve growth rates this year and next year as last in the high economy ten years ago. Development is also similar in other EU countries. Today, an EU economic growth of 2.8 percent and a 3 percent in 1999 is expected. This is more than the US, Japan and OECD can expect. "Europe is back." – Austria is back, so an American newspaper has recently had to admit jealously. The Federal Government's economic report, which I am allowed to present to you today together with my colleague, Mr Farnleitner, contains a comprehensive and, like me, impressive understanding of our economic and economic performance in the past year and also gives an outlook on the challenges ahead of us. I'm convinced that I'm venerationful Ladies and gentlemen, Austria as a whole has an excellent basis for a good path towards an economically successful and socially secure future.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-07-09-020-XX-NRSITZ-00135_d7e3462	Mr President. High house! After the day of celebration on the occasion of the presidency's adoption, it is now about asking and defining in the European Union – as my honourable previous speaker has said – what the new European framework conditions for the design of economic policy in the coming years mean. In particular, in the debate on the question of what is to be done in Brussels, in the countries and regions, it should not be overlooked that today the Europe of the 15 is a system of highly different countries, not only of different cultures but also of completely different economic dynamics. In recent years, especially since we have been doing this; this is always to be underlined – two new instruments have been increasingly introduced into economic policy; these English expressions have become standard: Best practices and benchmarking. It is crucial that many countries have tried in a very different way to deal with the problems of the same type in all countries, and it is only logical that it is therefore tried to compare the best recipe and perhaps to apply it itself. This is a dimension of European economic policy that will become more important in the next few years. This can be achieved by drawing regular comparisons between policies, such as, for the first time, in the field of employment policy, the Vienna Summit on the evaluation of the National Action Plans for Employment. If we consistently benefit from this instrument and ask ourselves less who does what when, then we will have to enrich economic policy in the European area. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] High house! Especially at a time when more and more international analyses are asking themselves the questions when and under what circumstances it could be possible not only to limit the Asian crisis but to turn it back into a growth process, it is necessary to ask the question of the quality of the European Union's location. I would like to tell you here that, in my view, Europe is regularly undervalued in the international comparison. If we compare the economic indicators of the economic areas, it becomes clear that the best regional economic area of those Western Europe is. Ladies and gentlemen! In contrast to the United States, Western Europe has a huge trade balance, a surplus of 169 billion US dollars in 1997, and a current account surplus of 125 billion US dollars. In comparison: USA: minus 198 billion dollars or 166 billion dollars. We should therefore not overlook the fact that the global economic location of Europe as a whole is favourable. This should enable us to deal more and more with the problems that are still pending in the face of a positive background. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] It is not only the economic area of Europe that is growing more than the other economic areas, but we have to do with the lucky fact that we live on the edge of a growth region in the northern and eastern neighbouring countries. In this area, where about 100 million people live, in the next few years, especially by means of the financial assistance that the European Union will provide in this area – in the Agenda 2000 something like a Marshall plan, aid in the double level – growth rates between 4 and 6 percent will be established, from which Austria will benefit in a special way through its extremely dense economic interdependence. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] In addition, the improvement of economic development in Germany is also an important point for us, because – I said this on other occasions in Munich –: If Bayern is fine, Austria is better. [[New applause with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] [[Abg. May. Schweitzer: What do you mean?]] Ladies and gentlemen! If we ask who will be part of the next few years, it is against this background that we can assume that we are going into a European age, but not in one dominated by markets, as we have read in literature many years ago. Let's come to ourselves! Ladies and gentlemen! The amazing development in the international comparison, presented by Mr Edlinger, first and foremost, as well as the economic research institutes, leads us to a particularly dynamic growth process in the export industry. I said from this point of view two years ago that we need a single market offensive in export because we have the biggest trade deficit with countries of the European Union. The figures you see in my report speak volumes: Austria's external trade with or delivered to the internal market has increased more than the internal market deliveries from other countries, i.e. by 13 percent in 1997, and we have also recorded a 10 percent increase in the first quarter of this year. In terms of the trade balance, this means that in 1997 we were able to improve the trade balance in the European area, i.e. in the EU, by 11 billion schilling and by a further 4 billion shillings in the first three months of this year alone. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] That means: Austria's economy is increasingly aware of the opportunities of the internal market, and we should do everything we can to improve it through the framework of the European internal market. Ladies and gentlemen! A particularly encouraging result is the external trade with Eastern Europe. Austria's link with these parts of the world is the highest of OECD. In the export sector – we have more than 17 percent market shares here – this also means that after an increase of almost 35 percent last year, we have a balance of payments of almost 40 billion schilling. This also makes it clear that in all discussions on future relations with these countries we have to deal with fears and expectations carefully. We still have much to gain in these countries in the next few years if the process continues as it appears. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Let me come to a general part of the assessment of the site Austria. It is true, ladies and gentlemen, that after decades of economic policy activity, it is possible to say that Austria's economic enterprise today is faced with framework conditions that previous generations could only dream of. I recall that today, not least thanks to the euro, we are faced with a situation characterized by low inflation, very low interest rates, low corporate taxes and free market access to all neighbouring markets, while increasing innovation and flexibility are improving our location quality. Contrary to many unacquainted calls, the location of Austria in the debate of investors is far better than in the published opinion. I can say this from the experiences of the last days, but above all from the experience of the ABA, the Austrian Business Agency, a company in front of my ministry. We have had the best year of settlement in the last year for decades, and we have all the conditions in the first half of this year alone that we can re-establish investments and jobs in about the same size. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Especially in the last few days, a number of large investors have also been in my house to announce that they will be investing in Austria again and permanently. In concrete terms, this is about the new Mercedes Chrysler Group, about Magna Europe, which has clear plans to further deepen its current activities here in Austria and, above all, to greatly expand the field of research in Austria. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Under these conditions, ladies and gentlemen, economic policy debate must focus on the increasingly clear competitive advantage in the modern discussion of different social systems of the market economy, namely what is called the craft skills of the population. In future, the key factor in comparison with location quality is less promotion, less taxes, connection fees and the like, but the quality of employees. And that's the call to this house, too. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Let me tell you, Mr President, an anecdote from the last few days. [[Abg. May. Schweitzer: May I get my secretary?]] – Correspondence, colleague! [[Abg. May. Schweitzer: I'll get my secretary!]] If you want it. [[New applause with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] This example may show that this is perhaps more important than some comparisons of numbers. [[Abg. May. Schweitzer: The engine is made by liberal professionals!]] Ladies and gentlemen! But this does not change the fact that, when we talk about skills, we also need to talk about problems in our labour market. We, Mr Hostash, and I, have composed ourselves and have had to deal, above all, with the fact that about 40 per cent of the Austrian unemployed are constantly being taken up by the same workers. This means that a number of economic sectors are clearly outlining their cyclic fluctuations in the economy to the labour market. This also means that, in connection with our national employment plans, we also have to address some taboo issues of the labour market beyond traditional barriers, because what is happening there is obvious. Ladies and gentlemen! If we take lifelong learning seriously with continuous further qualification, we also have to deal with new instruments. I only remember the bonus malus system proposed by me elsewhere in the field of unemployment insurance. Some words on youth unemployment. In the field of youth unemployment, we have to deal with the phenomenon that, although a trend in apprenticeship training can be seen in the light of the fact that more people go to the apprenticeship market than to the educational schools, many of our existing apprenticeships are no longer the basis for a lifelong stay in these sectors due to the rapid technological change in the economy. Therefore, we – social partners such as the government – face the challenge of providing more basic knowledge in training and of promoting the willingness to continue lifelong education. From my point of view, it is therefore very positive that in the NAP, too, the social partners have come to know that two years of training can be achieved if it is ensured that the basis for further and higher qualifications is laid. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Mr Hostasch and I have recently agreed to speed up the procedures for the recognition and enforcement of new occupations during a discussion with the social partners' secretary-generals over the next few months, and, secondly, we also discussed the need to ensure that there are sufficient companies ready to recruit these apprentices in each new profession. Synthetic teaching does not bring any advantage. You will see that in the next few weeks we will be implementing 16 more new occupations, especially new careers in the direction of new technologies. Ladies and gentlemen! Another point: Technology policy. If skills and innovation, as this means, are the decisive factors of competition, then there must be one to think of when, like Loch Ness, Austria is constantly publishing the concept of the research gap just before the summer season. In the next few days, a Technology Promotion Act - Mr One, and I am responsible for it - is going to be reviewed, as well as a Steering Committee for the Coordination of the relevant Ministries and, finally, a Fund, called KIR, will be set up new funding for government innovation and technology. It has been very often discussed here in this House that more needs to be done. Yes, more will happen, but only if we have more companies, especially large companies that are researching in this country. One of the priorities of my house is therefore to agree with all major investors in Austria that they may either start or strengthen research activities. That's from General Motors to Chrysler, as I called it to you. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] However, it is also important to facilitate access to technological innovation to small and medium-sized enterprises through advisory institutions, such as BIT in the field of the Chambers of Commerce and Ministries. For financing: Ladies and gentlemen! In the last few days, it has been ensured that the FFF which I am responsible for will receive an extra 470 million schilling from the budget as well as funds from my house, so that he can expect another grant of 630 million schilling this year. In addition, Mr Edlinger and I have agreed to equip the fund with a liability framework of 2 billion schilling. This means that there are no financial problems here and that 16 per cent of the demand can be covered entirely and without difficulties. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] In this context, let me once again clean up with another Mar. It is common to always measure Austria's research performance by how many patents are applied for per capita. Here we are not at the top of Europe. But in a discussion of the relevant European experts, we have come to the conclusion that the potential of innovation must be measured by two criteria: the number of patents and the number of patterns of use applied for, because the utility models are those that are most rapidly implemented on the market. If one takes the number of patents and utility models, Austria is clearly at the forefront of Sweden and France in Europe. I take this opportunity to present this perspective to you. We are willing to explain this once in internal discussions with representatives of the clubs by gentlemen of the Patent Office and my experts, so that we may perhaps reach a clear consensus. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! A permanent discussion in this country is: Are the entrepreneurs going out, or do we have enough companies? It's perfectly clear, we have a lower self-employed rate. And if we want to increase the rate of self-employed in Austria, this is not to do with government apples or support programmes alone, but it is also about removing mental barriers established for decades. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] From my experience I can tell you that many of the Austrian new entrepreneurs are actually older people. It is true that in Austria we have four categories of new entrepreneurs. We have entrepreneurs from vocation – this will in any case become entrepreneurs, even if it has failed twice before. We have entrepreneurs from Force – these are many older people, as they were 50, 55 years old, in the early sixties, who are praised in their occupations from the companies with large executions and then are independent as a business consultant or service contractor and are sponsored by the companies for another, two years with orders. But we also have relatively many entrepreneurs from despair – for example, when the unemployed have tried over years to integrate into the labour market and as the last step towards self-employment. This evening, when awarding the State Employment Prize, we have indeed found it very proud that there are unemployed companies, cooperatives, such as the Steyrer initiatives, which in a few years have become real local growth companies. If we could once bring this message, one must not become entrepreneurs out of despair or hopelessness in Austria, especially with entrepreneurship teachers at universities, this would be a great breakthrough. We have a lot of auxiliary farmers. 70 percent of our farmers are by-catches farmers, who are of course no longer included in the statistics as workers in agriculture. But they think like farmers and also work in agriculture. Despite this increase in final production, however, income per person employed in agriculture fell by 2.5 percent in real terms. How did this come about? The main reason for this is the planned reduction in degressive compensatory payments, which we have known since 1995. Indeed, for a period of four years, degressive payments have been agreed, so to speak, to suspend accession to the EU in agriculture. These have been 2.2 billion lower than a year earlier, but also, and this is more pleasing for agriculture in itself, the departure in agriculture has since the mid-1990s gone down to about half compared to the beginning of the 1990s, at which time the departure was already lower than it had been in about the 1980s. This does not mean, however, that the farmers all leave agriculture as quickly as possible, but migration has rather declined. The value of agriculture is always measured in gross domestic product. However, many agricultural services are not covered statistically, such as the care of the cultural landscape. After all, 80 percent of the Austrian total area is maintained up to the glacier regions of farmers – as a basis also for the holiday of relaxation, for tourism in Austria, but also for the provision of the population. At no time in Austria has the farmers covered the table of the people to such an extent and in such good quality, and we would be able to feed not only 8 million Austrians, but we would be able to feed 10 or more million Austrians. It's really a farmer's achievement. Thus, in agriculture, productivity has increased more in recent decades than it has been in industry. However, it cannot be denied that agricultural reforms are necessary. Overall, production in Europe, of course, increases by around 2 per cent per year depending on the weather. The population is no longer growing, and the Europeans are relatively well fed. So there is little room here. That is, we are increasingly dependent on the world market. But with the conditions we have in Europe, for example with the environmental conditions, with the animal welfare requirements, also with the social conditions to which we confess, we cannot compete with world market prices. That is, we need reasonable rules, and I hope that within the framework of Agenda 2000 there will be arrangements within a year which farmers can also accept.

That means that there is a chaotic state of acceptance of liability – and that just before the end of the summer break of Parliament, which will no longer come together. And in September, Klagenfurt must already submit his application. Without a constitutional takeover of guarantees, it is completely excluded that Klagenfurt and Carinthia have an opportunity to be accepted as candidates at the IOC. That is why we have tabled this request. However, I would also like to draw the attention of the ladies and gentlemen of the government parties to the fact that § 66 of the Federal Budget Act, under the title of federal liability, requires: § 66 (1): A liability (guarantee) according to § 1346f ABGB or warranty of the federal government may only take over the Federal Minister of Finance. The latter may assume liability only in accordance with the powers contained in the Federal Finance Act or in a special federal law within the meaning of Article 42(5) of the B-VG, and in particular must ensure that liability is assumed only in respect of obligations relating to projects specified in the relevant legal authorisation. The Austrian Government's plan to assume these responsibilities only by a government decision is first of all non-constitutional, second legally non-binding, and thirdly, it presents the danger that the offer and the candidate of Klagenfurt for the Olympic Games will not be accepted as conforming to the IOC. This is the reason why we take this initiative and take the lead, to which we also invite colleagues from the other parties to participate, because basically it is not a question of whether the opposition or the government is making this request, but rather of a constitutional, legally binding regulation of the acceptance of liability, which, in the event that it is not taking place today, is seriously at risk of the Olympic Games and the candidacy of Klagenfurt and Carinthia. I also call on Mr Leikam, who is aware of this situation, to convince his colleagues in the government parties that we now have the opportunity and should no longer allow ourselves to accept that this problem could be solved by a non-binding decision of the Federal Government. We have made this provision for the 1999 budget, which we now have to decide so that we can demonstrate to the IOC the constitutional state, for otherwise we will be told: Dear friends, these are empty kilometers! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Not even the provisions laid down in the Constitution and in budgetary law are complied with. ParlaMint-AT_1998-07-09-020-XX-NRSITZ-00135_d7e10834	Ladies and gentlemen! Mr President. I agree to answer the urgent question raised by the Green Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). However, I would like to make a few general remarks, which go beyond the specific context, but which I think are important. The aim is to highlight the Austrian asylum policy in principle. I am convinced that with figures and facts I can prove that Austria has a leading role in the protection of refugees within the international community and within Europe. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Austria may, for example, increase by 56 percent for asylum seekers over the previous year. This is an increase that does not exist in any other EU Member State to that extent. On the contrary: In most other EU countries, the number of asylum seekers even declined or is largely constant. The example of Germany shows that the number of asylum seekers in Austria has clearly declined. Ladies and gentlemen! It is therefore not possible to say that Austria would steal itself from its responsibility, to which I would like to confess myself very clearly here and today. This becomes even more evident when comparing the numbers of asylum seekers with those of our eastern and southern neighbours. Austria had around 7 000 asylum seekers last year, for Hungary the UNHCR has 1 000 applications, for Italy 1 900 applications and for the Czech Republic 2 100. This makes it clear that we bear the main responsibility, not only in comparison to our eastern neighbours, but also to our southern neighbours. If I compare this with other European countries, it can be seen that Austria is leading the asylum figures. Germany has the largest share of 51 percent... [[Abg. Oils: With the applicants, not with those who have asylum!]] [[Abg. Smolle: Applicants – this is not art! It is true that there are a lot of people coming here!]] I will allow myself to address this argument as soon as possible. With the applicants, Germany is clearly at the top of almost 52 percent, followed by the UK and the Netherlands, and then Austria is already coming to France with a share of more than 3 percent. Large and rich countries in Europe have a smaller share: Denmark 2.5 percent, Spain 2 percent, Sweden 2 percent, Greece 0.7%, Ireland 0.52 percent, Finland 0.31 percent, Italy only 0.24 percent, Luxembourg 0.12 percent and Portugal only 0.12 percent. I believe that these figures show very impressively that Austria has a very clear and good position with the asylum seekers. We talked about it yesterday – and I still think it is necessary to repeat it here – that we have recorded 92 000 Bosnian refugees in recent years. We have integrated more than 66 000 of these into Austria, and we have spent more than 5 billion schillings since 1992. And what's even more important: Together with the countries and the various aid organisations, we have established a network of assistance, integration and the possibility of return. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen of the Liberals and Greens! Over the last ten years, Austria has received well over 100 000 asylum seekers and has carried out the necessary procedures. During this period, more than 100 000 asylum seekers recognized more than 10 000 as political refugees. This is a percentage of just over 10 percent, which is well above the average of the percentages of all other European countries. In the statistics I have available, Austria is fourth or fifth place in the recognition rate among the 15 EU Member States. That is, the role of Austria as a country in which people apply for asylum and in which they also have the opportunity to gain recognition is one that goes far beyond what is the case in other countries. I would also like to stress that the number of asylum seekers has increased significantly in the first half of the year. If we still had around 2,600 asylum seekers in Austria during the same period last year, this figure rose to 4,528 in the first half of 1998. This is an increase of 56 percent. No other country within the European Union has seen such an increase. I believe that this increase alone will show that, from the point of view of the asylum seekers, Austria is still a very attractive country of asylum. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Austria was also active in refugee aid during the first hour in connection with the situation in Kosovo. As early as early as June this year, the Council of Ministers has already made available an emergency humanitarian aid of 5 million schillings for the support of displaced people in Macedonia, northern Albania and Kosovo itself. The use of these funds is carried out through non-governmental organisations. We have made 5 million Schilling available, while I know that the EU Commission has also made the same amount available. This also shows that we are in a leading position. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! It is not true that we have a restrictive asylum law. It's the opposite! If we look at the asylum laws adopted in our European neighbours and compare them with the Austrian asylum law adopted a year ago, it is clear that Austria has an asylum law that is much more liberal, much more open than the asylum laws of other European countries. I would just like to add a few examples, which I believe were significant and good improvements that Austria adopted in the new Asly Act 1997. First of all, it has decided that the third-country clause, which you are fighting in this way, does not apply to the past, but to the future. This means that a man or a woman can only be returned to a safe third country if it is ensured in the future that they actually receive an asylum procedure in that safe third country, if, second, they are guaranteed that they will be entitled to stay in that country during this procedure and third, that they are protected from repatriation in the country of origin. Secondly, in the asylum law of last year, we decided that asylum at the airport would be included in UNHCR and that there should be no decision against UNHCR. As a result, for example, the number of applicants for asylum at Schwechat Airport increased in the first half of 1998. Thirdly, In the new asylum law, we have provided the possibility that in addition to political, racial and religious reasons for the persecution, rape is also recognised as a reason for persecution and thus as a reason for escape. Fourth: We have provided the opportunity to seek asylum at the border. This possibility is admittedly only used to a small extent at the moment, but there is an opportunity to do so. It has established its own Integration Advisory Board in the Ministry of the Interior, which has the task of consulting the Minister of the Interior and approving, in severe cases, the so-called humanitarian residence right. We have also created an independent, non-wisdoming authority, namely the Bundesasylsenat. He works in the second instance and is in no connection with the Ministry of the Interior. The activity of this independent federal asylsenate is such that it has also been considered by the opposition parties – by the Greens and the Liberals – at least as neutral, if not as positive. In the new asylum law, we have also met a number of others, in my opinion very important conditions. This includes, for example, a provisional residence permit if a person has entered Austria in accordance with the law. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! I have already said clearly at the beginning of the debate in Parliament that after a year of practice the new asylum law will be discussed again by all concerned, including the various non-governmental organizations, and that we will make necessary adaptations due to the experience we have in practice in the first year with the new asylum law, and I will make concrete proposals. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Today, ladies and gentlemen, the Constitutional Court has announced that, on the basis of a complaint from the independent Bundesasylsenate, it has abolished a deadline in the new asylum law, which you have always criticised, and today an initiative proposal will be submitted to Parliament, which will take account of this statement of the Constitutional Court. I believe that we have succeeded in achieving by means of the new asylum law that Austria is a country that will continue to provide help and protection for people who are persecuted for various reasons, whether political, religious or ethnic. Otherwise, ladies and gentlemen, the situation in the asylum field in other European countries is looking forward to. For example, just a few days ago, Switzerland decided that foreigners who enter Switzerland without papers or stay illegally in Switzerland can no longer apply for asylum. [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: That's right anyway!]] Germany has reduced the social benefits to asylum seekers. Italy has tightened the rules on the detention of asylum seekers. Denmark has adopted a whole package of restrictive measures. This direct comparison, my dear friends Ladies and gentlemen, I think it is impressive that Austria is a country which has designed the asylum law in such a way that it is in the interests of those people who are really persecuted, while other European states have carried out a drastic and distinct strengthening that I do not even think I am doing in any form in Austria. [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Schwarzenberger.]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! For me, the burden balance, as Mrs Stoisits has called it, is also very important. But I do not see it as a burden compensation, but as a solidarity compensation. I will try to ensure that a decisive step is taken in a good and right direction during the Austrian Presidency in the European Union with this solidarity balance. Together with the EU Commission, we present these days two drafts for a joint action aimed at establishing solidarity between the status of temporary persons and persons fleeing from mass movements within the European Union. I think that is very important. It is not only a question of financial compensation between the European States but also of compensation for persons, so that it is ensured that not a country alone must bear the burden. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] I also say very openly to you: I am very pleased to welcome a suggestion from the Liberal Forum, which has been made today in a press release, in which the Liberal Forum believes that this burden-balance should be extended not only within the European Union, but also to EU accession candidates, at least on those of the first round, because I believe that this is useful and correct, and I will endeavour to achieve that our immediate neighbours who want to become members of the European Union will also be included in this solidarity burden compensation. ParlaMint-AT_1998-07-09-020-XX-NRSITZ-00135_d7e11104	Ladies and gentlemen! On the individual questions. Question 1: The foreign police statistics conducted in accordance with EU standards show only nationality and no ethnic groups. That is why I can only give you information about Yugoslavian nationals, but not specific information on Kosovo Albanians. Under this premise, I can tell you that in the period from 1st century to 1st. Years until 31. In May 1998, the following total figures are announced for abducted or withdrawn Yugoslav citizens: On the land road, 616 persons were moved, 154 people were moved back and forth on the airway. In the first six months of 1998, some 4,600 asylum applications were submitted. Of these 4,600 asylum seekers, around 1 300 come from Yugoslavia, and according to our calculations and estimates, about 80 percent of them are from Kosovo. On question 2: A separate statistics on refusals to Hungary are not available and cannot be prepared in the short term. At the Austrian border crossing points were in the period 1st. Years until 31. On 1 May 1998, a total of 1,238 Yugoslav citizens were rejected for a variety of reasons. At the border crossing points of the Burgenland there were a total of 584. During that period, 302 Yugoslav nationals were resettled to Hungary under the Austrian-Hungarian Shift Agreement. Question 3: The conditions for deportations, reschedulings and rescindings to Hungary are laid down in both the Foreign Act and the bilateral rescheduling agreement with Hungary. In this context, I expressly point out that, under the Mob Agreement with Hungary, transit may not be carried out if the person concerned "is in danger of being subjected to inhuman treatment or the death penalty, or in his life or his freedom for reasons of race, religion, nationality, belonging to a particular social group or his political views." This clause in Article 4 of the Agreement is a clause which the Austrian legislator has already approved in many thrust agreements. Furthermore, Mrs Stoisits, the statement of the question is not true that the UNHCR has found that Hungary is not a safe third country in principle. According to the UNHCR, as I see it and as I have been informed, in specific cases and in certain constellations, a third country security cannot be assumed from the outset. Questions 4 and 5: I would like to point out once again that it is my firm conviction that, on account of day-to-day opportunities, there is no way to make value judgments on a neighboring country, nor to make value judgments that I do not think are justified in any way. Hungary is a secure third country for the Austrian Federal Government. Hungary is a member of the Council of Europe and has been subject to the full control of the Council of Europe's human rights provisions. [[Abg. May. Stoisits: Yugoslavia is also a member of the Council of Europe!]] In Hungary, not only the European Convention on Human Rights but also the right of individual complaint is fully available in the event of alleged violations of this Convention. Hungary has ratified the Geneva Convention on Refugees and has with 1. In March of this year, every reservation to the Convention has also been withdrawn. Hungary therefore fulfils the Refugee Convention, as all other Western European states do. Hungary has clear rules on the protection of refugees, the protection of deportation in its asylum law and in its foreign law. The UNHCR, ladies and gentlemen, has also repeatedly praised the new Hungarian legislation. In my opinion, Hungary has a corresponding asylum system, which also maintains a check-out by courts. In addition, there is a UNHCR representative in Hungary who works very closely with the Hungarian authorities. On question 6: Austria sees no reason to qualify Hungary as an unsafe country, but is very pleased with the indisputably positive development of the Hungarian legal system in recent years. There is therefore no reason to treat Hungary differently from other neighbouring countries in foreign law and, in general, to refrain from deportations and relocations to Hungary. Questions 7 and 8: As you know, the new asylum law also created the independent Bundesasylsenat. The demand for this authority to initiate its internal territory and for complete independence was raised particularly vehemently by, among other things, the Greens and also supported by me as Minister. It is therefore very difficult for me, as Minister of the Interior, to address the accusations raised in these two questions, because I have no competence in this regard. However, I would like to point out that, at a transitional stage, it is undoubtedly difficult to achieve a single Judicatur, even if it is a single judge's decision. But I am convinced that there will be an approximation of the Judicture in this important question in the near future. On the question 9: The conditions of § 57 Foreign Law are material conditions to be assessed in accordance with the circumstances of the individual case. For good reasons, the law does not provide that in some countries it should not be postponed at all. In this respect, the Minister of the Interior would not have any jurisdiction to declare a general ban on deportation to a particular state. It will therefore be necessary to examine, in each individual case, whether, in the event of deportation, the person concerned is at risk of inhuman treatment or punishment, or whether life or freedom due to belonging to a particular group of people or because of political views is at risk. If the test confirms such a risk, it must not be postponed or postponed. This is the result of the law, and this is also clearly dependent on me. That is why, ladies and gentlemen, I believe that it is necessary and important to distinguish very strongly and that decisions are made, not in general, but only in the circumstances of the individual case. And such decisions cannot require any general instruction. Question 10: Last night I received a fax from the Secretary-General of Amnesty International. It contains the presentation of a matter of March this year. I have, of course, led to the review of this matter. However, I have not yet received a final opinion on this review, especially because the information is not very accurate. As soon as I have a final opinion – including an opinion from the Hungarian authorities, because this statement refers essentially to events that took place in Hungary – I will notify you accordingly. On the question 11: I can only take consequences if I have carefully examined the case. The examination of this case has not yet been completed. That's why I can't take any consequences from it. Question 12: The granting of a temporary right of residence for persons leaving their home country due to war disputes is a question which I do not believe should be resolved by a country alone and cannot be solved at the present state of European integration. I think it is necessary and important that the States of the European Union work together. I can assure you that I am working fully on such a common procedure, because I believe that this is not only an operation in the interests of the security of our country and in the interests of the people of our country, but also very, very important for people affected by such military events. I will therefore, together with the Commission, ensure that such a solidarity is achieved within the European Union as soon as possible for those who need temporary protection. I believe that there should be no single entry of Austria, but that it is absolutely necessary to find such a solid balance in a broad accord between the individual states of the European Union. That, ladies and gentlemen, was my answers soon. Mr President, I would have liked to say more about this.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-07-09-020-XX-NRSITZ-00135_d7e14455	Thank you, Mr President. - Dear Members! The Civil Service Law Novelle to be treated today should first bring clarity and transparency, but also administrative simplification, both for workers and for the service provider. I would like to draw up a number of points, in particular the creation of a central medical service at the Federal Pension Office. The responsibility of the staff's pension insurance institution for medical examination in the incapacity procedure had to be discontinued as a result of a finding by the Administrative Court of Justice of July 1997. That condition, which we previously believed to eliminate, namely different assessment practice, such as among officials through the review by official doctors, was thereby restored. This has recently led to a different standard of access to the service incapacity board. The aim is to establish a central medical assessment body at the Federal Pensions Office with a view to achieving a uniform standard of assessment. In the future, a chief physician will carry out a uniform review with competent doctors who also have special training in the field of occupational health. If, in certain cases, additional expert advice is required, it is also necessary to appoint experts from the various regions, depending on where the official or the official comes from, to draw up an opinion. This is, as has been said, sent to this Federal Pension Office for the submission to the chief physician, who will then be on the subject. I think this is a very good way to go, as it is also possible to produce decentralised opinions, but on the other hand there is a job that can provide uniform access for all federal staff to obtain the service incapacity pension. – That would be the first essential point! Another important point, in my opinion, is also the change to the travel fee regulation in § 39, by which the administration is simplified in so far, as general army officials who provide executive services and are mainly active in the field of foreign services, no longer have to make individual payments in the future, but are paid monthly in the form of flat-rate remuneration. This makes sense because it brings administrative simplification both to the service provider's government and to officials in the executive service. For reasons of time and time discipline, I would like to conclude my speech. Thank you.

This is an anachronism of the special kind. It cannot be a priority or at all task of a Federal Minister or a Federal Minister to vote or to vote on nine museum directors. This is where politics has to impose a self-restriction and finally the umbilical cord between the political influence and the museums to interrupt or cut off the museum directors. Another criticism of the liberal opposition to the institution model is the fact that museums only gain legal personality when they give themselves a museum order. For this museum arrangement, individual museums have time until the year 2003 according to the corresponding season scheme. Noteing that in today's short-lived period this is an inadequately long period of time, even though you are now talking about an extension of the legislative period, it is recognized that the museum reform has been transferred to the Sankt-Nimmerleins day at least in subareas. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Another criticism is that this point is still not rehabilitated and at the same time constitutes a violation of the principle of legality, which is laid down in the law that either one or two managing directors should be used. This is a clear violation of the principle of legality, because: Who determines whether to use one or two managing directors? Does that help depending on the size, the square meters of exhibition space or the number of employees? It is an undetermined concept of law. I would just like to mention the reliability, care and security of money here. But, of course, it also had something of compulsiveness and slowness. It is no longer modern – it has also been recognized. I would like to quote here the Öhlinger and more Corinth quoted by you. They have voted in favour of an institution of public law because it affects conservation and research as well as, in part, non-university research institutions, in the fields of art history, architecture, natural sciences and technical development, as well as the associated auxiliary sciences such as restoration and new conservation techniques. Austria has a good reputation to defend. Because of these special tasks, the Federal Government has decided today – hopefully also the Parliament will do it – for a public-law institution. The partial right introduced in 1988 has enabled individual areas to become competitive. It has given the museums the opportunity to break out of the districts of the dusted cultural temples and partly of the public hostility – I remember very carefully – and to become new, modern exhibition and communication centres. The new path, which started in 1988 with the partial right, has proved itself in large parts. Now is the next step. What has brought about partial justice? – Better numbers of visitors, better exhibitions in the sense of greater attractiveness of the exhibitions, the new exhibitions and the new suspensions, important publications of these museums, new purchases – also by means of partial rights, which is noted here. Justice is therefore a logical continuation and certainly not – in this I want to give Krüger a right – the last step on the journey of the Federal Museums. That means first of all: The Republic confesses itself to its museums and to the responsibility it carries for the museums. Secondly, it means extending the positive possibility of partial justice to all areas of museums, as I have already said. Thirdly, there is the resolution of the financial conduct from the camera list with an effective controlling instrument. Fourthly, this represents a far greater autonomy of the directors of the houses and greater flexibility. Financially, the whole thing is secured. That's already in the law, and there's an escape clause. I would just like to say: The ingredients for better economics and for more market in this area are present, with this law being laid the foundation stone for this. The employees, the directors and the directors only need to benefit from them and prove a new quality in the management of these houses. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-07-09-020-XX-NRSITZ-00135_d7e19906	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! In principle, I welcome the current Federal Museum Act, which makes the Austrian Federal Museums a scientific institution of public law with its own legal personality. This form of organization, which is linked to the positive experience gained with the partial right, is now creating the conditions for our museums to present modern and customer-oriented. I also welcome the choice of a model which allows for the scope of design and increases the autonomy of the individual museums, but without completely removing the influence of the Federal Ministry of Education and Art. I am basically different from my colleague Krüger on this point of view. I insist that the responsibility must continue to remain with the Ministry, because there are invaluable values with the collection material in the museums for the Republic of Austria and because it also involves a cultural contract. It is necessary that relevant social developments in the field of technology continue in the future – by the way, I would like to ask in this context: What about the Technical Museum? I mean, right now we would need its opening! – which are taken up by natural sciences and the arts and made understandable and accessible to a broad public. This is very important to us! That is why the state is investing a lot of money in this area, and that is why the policy here should continue to keep the leadership. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] What are the main objectives of this reform? – As has already been said, more opportunities for self-initiative, more autonomy in the cultural and artistic field, economic independence in personnel and budget issues, thus also increasing mobility and, in conjunction with it, better use of resources and more efficiency. I would now like to mention three points which were particularly important to the Socialist Group and which we have therefore advocated in this law. Firstly, the law also formulated a cultural policy mission for the Federal Museums. I would like the Austrian museums to develop their own image and identity more than before, thus becoming unmistakable, as is the case with the Guggenheim Museum or with the Secession. I think there are very good and positive approaches to this. Previously, the Volkskundemuseum was addressed: Of course, this very small museum has no very large budget. But for example, with the Bhutan exhibition, this evening an exhibition was successful, through which this museum became known beyond the borders of Austria. For the first time, it has succeeded in bringing exhibits from the Kingdom of Bhutan to Austria or even to the West. This has been achieved because there is good cooperation in the area of development cooperation between the Kingdom of Bhutan and Austria. I'm very happy about that. Of course, our museum of folklore cannot compete with a museum of folklore in Amsterdam, but it can set accents very well, and it also sets them! I would like to give you a good example. The Art History Museum is important for a certain time horizon, especially for the art of the Dutch. With 1.45 million visitors, a visitor record was achieved at the Brueghel exhibition. This exhibition attracted a lot of audience from abroad. A tradition has continued: The museum itself has one of the most important collections of Brueghel and has become increasingly accepted in the context of this exhibition on this subject. I mean, we need museums as places of living encounter. In this context, consideration should be given to appropriate opening hours and appropriate prices for people who do not have very high incomes. These questions must be addressed in the future. You have to deal with new content and sometimes also overcome the purely museum character. The director of the Sigmund-Freud Museum, whom I would like to address as a particularly attractive museum, Mrs Scholz-Straßer, has formulated this very aptly. She said: A museum, it may also be so lively, dynamic, didactic, hypermodern and super attractive, remains a museum. In some areas, to stay with Sigmund Freud, scientific work is of great importance. The collection, on the other hand, remains of subordinate priority. The second point we have made is that, in the context of the reform of the museums, staff must also be involved, that curators and scientific staff should be involved in a new concept and also have a right of participation. Thirdly, we have insisted that Parliament should also be involved in the future discussion. That is what we have put forward in our own motion for a resolution in the Committee, which reaffirms this once again. In this sense, I am looking forward to a lively discussion of this subject in the future. Thank you.

Alvarez-Machain, held six to three votes in favour, that the violent abduction of an accused to the United States does not constitute an obstacle to the prosecution of that person. An accused person cannot be prosecuted in the United States only if the extradition contract to be applied had been violated. However, if an extradition contract does not expressly prohibit the violent abduction of an accused person, the American court could exercise its jurisdiction over the accused. The Supreme Court of the United States of America only had to assess whether the extradition treaty to be applied had been infringed, but not whether the violent abduction would have disregarded generally recognised principles of international law. Nor could these general principles of international law be used to interpret an extradition contract." – As far as the Austrian Minister of Justice is concerned. It concludes: "Austria has been negotiating a new extradition agreement with the United States of America since 1987." – This is the one we have for treatment today. It is also said in the answer – and this is particularly interesting – : "With regard to the decision of the Supreme Court of the United States of America mentioned above, the Austrian side will now be required to include a contractual provision in the new extradition contract, according to which the abduction of an accused by force or list to the United States for the purpose of prosecution or enforcement constitutes a violation of the extradition contract." – As far as the very well-founded promise of Michalek at the time. Now we have the new contract. And are kidnapping or torture excluded from the new contract? – No, they are not excluded! I think this is an intolerable result! Additional Protocol to the Convention on Human Rights and ultimately § 20 ARHG is not allowed to be extradited in the event of a serious death penalty. Thank you.
