ParlaMint-AT_1998-07-09-020-XX-NRSITZ-00135_d7e21464	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to begin by expressing the wish that the political groups will once again be able to give their contributions at the end of the agenda, and then, of course, under pressure of time, as part of the timing of the debates. The fact that it is supposed to go quickly is human understandable. However, problems arise when short summary statements have to be made in the last few minutes. I can say briefly on the right to take over: It is an exemplary law. It has already been announced that this law serves to significantly improve the conditions of the capital market in such a way that confidence-building measures are imposed on foreign investors, for those who invest widely in the various listed companies. This applies in particular to the area of the American and English pension funds. The political core with regard to the economic impact of the economy is the creation of an alternative to strategic movers and, ultimately, the creation of an alternative to that of Austrian decisions within Austria in the future, because such funds operate differently from strategic investors in terms of influence on companies. The law has been discussed for a long time, and various variants have been found. Finally, I think it is also positive that, under quotation marks, it has been "spected" in the form that the organisational structures – originally there was a Commission and an authority – have been brought together. There is now an authority which is responsible for a relatively broad spectrum and ensures maximum transparency in such transactions, both in the case of voluntary public takeover bids and in the field of obligations. I therefore believe that this is a significant step in the right direction. The fact that, in the final analysis, a regulatory authorisation will be provided to enable the Commission to determine the individual criteria in accordance with the framework laid down in the law will certainly be used to create quality in the sense that there are rules applicable to each individual case. I would like to conclude by thanking the ladies and gentlemen of the Ministry of Justice, Professor Doralt, who has been supporting us for a long time, for the really great contribution. - Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-07-09-020-XX-NRSITZ-00135_d7e21507	Mr Krüger is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). – Ladies and gentlemen, for you there is still a one-minute speaking time.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-07-09-020-XX-NRSITZ-00135_d7e21528	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to announce the criticism I have made in the working group against the provisions of the obligations offered here as well: Mandatory offers are to be made if a controlling interest in the target company is to be acquired. My criticism is against the fact that the legislator has failed to define the conditions under which control participation is possible. In this way, hot iron will be passed on to a takeover commission in the form of a regulatory authorization. In my opinion, this is a legitimate misappropriation. Rather, the legislator should be able to determine the conditions under which control participation is to be held, and the Commission should decide in individual cases whether or not this controlling participation is actually acquired. We know from the syndicate agreements et cetera that, under special circumstances, there is a duty to offer, since there are dominant influences.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-07-09-020-XX-NRSITZ-00135_d7e21658	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! I would also like to ask you to pay a little attention to this item on the agenda. I believe that this law is a milestone in the development of social and capital market law and that, together with the stock law adapted to EU law, the Securities Supervision Act and the recently updated Stock Exchange Act, we now have truly modern social and capital market conditions that meet the needs of investors for security and fairness, thereby promoting and facilitating the provision of capital for Austrian companies. I too would like to thank Professor Doralt for the infinite support he has given us. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] The general rules of play for all takeover bids to expire in fair conditions in the future were not controversial. Controversy, on the other hand, has been discussed for a very long time with regard to the two points which have already been raised today, namely: When is there a controlling participation, and at what price is the assignment to be made? . I believe that it is well justified to leave the question to be assessed very much according to the circumstances of the individual case, when there is a controlling participation, a dominant influence, a regulation by regulation, as this may be much more flexible. With regard to the price, it was essentially a question of whether the minority shareholders should also be involved in the so-called control bonus or package surcharge. In this respect, there has been a compromise between the interests of minority shareholders on the one hand and the interests of the majority shareholders often found in Austrian stock companies, or at least core shareholders, in particular those who have acquired their participation in a package award themselves. The discount of 15 percent is not mandatory. This discount may be reduced or completely eliminated in the statutes. In this context, therefore, there will certainly be a "competition of the statutes". This law on takeover will bring Austria – and I really want to stress this – to the top of the European states with a balanced and sophisticated right of takeover. The step is also of European importance: Against the background of this Takeover Act, during our Presidency we can continue negotiations on the proposal for a Takeover Directive more credible than without national legislation. Even if this is a framework directive, it will require a certain amount of adjustment, but this is what we are consciously buying and what the first time a takeover right is to be introduced in Austria in the proposed form should not be terminated. Thank you.
